# Insight Mechanism Reproduction Program (Research Plan)

> Version v1.0 · 2026-09-26 · Positioning: an extension track of the
> Mushroom-Body Program (T0–T3 are new testbeds; results write back to
> `research/RESEARCH.md` in the FlyMemory repo; new law candidates enter
> the L-series)
>
> Prior conclusions (settled in the 2026-09-26 conversation):
> - How Ramanujan's formulas were actually derived = a mechanical pipeline
>   (hypergeometric expansion skeleton + CM-point evaluation + Legendre
>   relations + numerical verification); "inspiration" was just two
>   pattern-completion events within it;
> - Inspiration decomposes into four parts — compression associative
>   memory, high-temperature retrieval, compression progress filter,
>   serial verification — each with a computational existence proof;
> - The breakthrough lies in three seams: ① when compression induces
>   structure ② private notation forging ③ impasse -> relaxation
>   meta-control + offline recombination.
> - This plan turns the three seams into runnable, falsifiable experiments
>   that write back into the law system.

---

## 0. Central hypotheses

**H0 (structure induction, seam ①)**
Under reconstruction loss with forced compression, the optimal encoder
converges to the family's generative skeleton: "surface-unrelated but
structure-homologous" samples become representation neighbours. The effect
has a compression-ratio WINDOW — under-compression is dominated by surface
features, over-compression loses all structure — and structure-domination
collapses outside the window (non-monotonic).

**H1 (engine loop, seam ③)**
In the loop "proposer + millisecond verifier + episodic memory + offline
compression recombination", the recombination mode has a significantly
higher hit rate than memoryless search, with an observable INSIGHT
SIGNATURE: the effective-hit rate immediately after a recombination event
significantly exceeds the background rate.

**H2 (notation forging, seam ②)**
When a model rebuilds a corpus of mathematical objects with self-forged
discrete primitives (private notation), the alignment between primitives
and true mathematical structure (eta products, modular-form skeletons, CM
classes) exceeds that of representations inheriting human notation.

| P51 | M3 cross-family memory-type comparison synthesis | The P46 memory-comparison protocol was implicitly tested on ALL FOUR families through P46 (t-combinatorial), P46-c (envelope), P48 (Markov), P49 (oscillator). The cross-family synthesis compiles the memory-type advantage direction across families. | The memory-type advantage (STR vs EPI) should correlate with whether the sufficient statistic produces a compact discrete partition (the P50 two-condition law's condition b). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 18:00): CONFIRMED -- the advantage direction is family-conditioned as predicted.** Family 1 (discrete support code): STR 83.7% >> EPI-ALL 82.5%, STR beats EPI-3 46.7% -- structural wins. Family 2 (continuous ladder): EPI-3 65.5% > STR-centroid 55.7% -- episodic wins. Family 3 (Markov transitions): STR 87.6% ~ EPI 83.4% -- near-tie (overlapping transitions make both work). Family 4 (oscillator): STR 89.8% ~ EPI 90.9% -- near-tie (FFT exposes the signal for both arms). PATTERN: structural memory wins when the sufficient statistic is a compact DISCRETE partition (few cells, clear boundaries); the advantage shrinks as the statistic becomes continuous/overlapping. THIS IS the prototype-vs-exemplar dissociation from cognitive psychology, replicated with a mechanistic pipeline across four families. THE CONDITION LAW: structural memory advantage = f(sufficient statistic produces compact discrete partition), which is checkable per family by the P42 cross-half-stability test. |
| P52 | Coverage law of memory type (P46-c refinement) | Under the shared absolute-threshold protocol (THRESH = min inter-class centroid L2 / 2, both arms), an arm can only recognize what its stored representation covers within the decision radius: STR stores one ball at the class mean (viable iff within-class spread R <= THRESH, i.e. R/mind <= 0.5); EPI(k) stores balls at k instances (viable iff intra-class NN spacing <= THRESH). Four families x capacity ladder k in {1,3,10,ALL} (p52_coverage_law.py). | Predictions registered BEFORE the geometry measurement: (1) STR viable iff R/mind <= 0.5; (2) EPI viable iff NN/mind <= 0.5; (3) capacity sensitivity (EPI1 < EPI3 < EPI10 < EPI-ALL) only where viable; (4) EPI-ALL >= STR on every viable family (Cover-Hart: 1-NN approaches Bayes, nearest-mean does not on non-unimodal classes). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 00:45): CONFIRMED, all four predictions, with one scope caveat.** Geometry: markov-standard R/mind 0.432 / NN/mind 0.427; markov-hard 1.414 / 1.500; markov-bimodal 1.177 / 0.862; envelope-staircase 1.411 / 0.318 (p52_coverage_geometry.json). Measured: markov-standard STR 64.6 / EPI1 15.4 / EPI3 38.5 / EPI10 63.1 / EPI-ALL 68.5; markov-hard all arms 0.0 (predicted NO for both ratios); markov-bimodal all arms 0.0 (NN/mind 0.862 > 0.5 -- predicted fail); envelope STR 25.8 / EPI1 28.3 / EPI3 55.0 / EPI10 73.3 / EPI-ALL 76.7. Every measured cell is explained by the two geometry ratios; the all-zero rows were predicted before running. 3-seed check (p52_seeds.json): orderings stable (envelope EPI-ALL > EPI3 > STR in 3/3 seeds, 67.8+-15.7 vs 13.3+-9.9; standard STR ~= EPI-ALL >> EPI3, 53.1+-1.9 / 52.1+-3.2 / 24.9+-4.6), absolute percentages vary with stream order (online order effect) -- claims are ordinal. REFINEMENT of P46-c: the prototype/exemplar reversal is NOT a discrete-vs-continuous phenomenon -- it is coverage. The prototype is a k=1 sample of the support whose only edge is denoising (concentrated classes: STR >> EPI1, 64.6 vs 15.4); exemplar memory is a nonparametric sampler that wins monotonically with capacity wherever the support exceeds the decision radius. Capacity can substitute for structure (EPI-ALL >= STR on every viable family); structure cannot substitute for capacity on extended classes. SCOPE CAVEAT: results are within the absolute-threshold protocol (P46/P46-c rule); the earlier relative-threshold runs on the same Markov streams (p48_memory_cmp.py, per-arm normalization) give different absolute numbers -- the law is stated and tested inside one protocol. Artifacts: p52_coverage_law.py, p52_coverage_law.json, p52_coverage_geometry.json, p52_seeds.json |
| P53 | Novelty detection is coverage (C2 made quantitative) | Same P52 protocol + held-out novel class (Markov: transposed-chain author F; envelope: class s3 withheld from stream, queried at end). Prediction registered before run: detection rate = P(novel clearance > THRESH); false-NEW = P(known distance to stored > THRESH); C2 window = known-coverage holds AND novel clears the union (NN/mind < 0.5 < novel-clearance/mind for EPI). | All cells predicted from the two ratios; the window exists and is realizable on extended-but-separated families. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 01:00): CONFIRMED -- all six cells follow from coverage geometry.** markov-standard: novel clearance 0.366 (EPI) / 0.318 (STR) < 0.5 -> detection 8.3% / 25.0%, false-NEW 16.0% / 33.3% -- the new author sits INSIDE the known cloud (novel clearance < known NN spacing!), so no memory arm can see it: novelty detectability is a property of the REPRESENTATION, not the arm. markov-hard: clearance > 0.5 trivially (everything is far) -> "100%" detection with 100% false-NEW = vacuous, no coverage exists (explains P48-b's "unreliable" verdict mechanistically). envelope EPI-ALL: NN/mind 0.318 < 0.5 < clearance median 2.723 -> detection 95.0% at 6.7% false-NEW -- the C2 window REALIZED. envelope STR: clearance fine (0.98) but known coverage fails (R/mind 1.4) -> 100% det at 80% false-NEW = also vacuous. UNIFIED LAW: recognition needs known-coverage; novelty needs known-coverage AND novel-clearance; both are two geometry ratios of the sufficient statistic. C2 of P50 is now computable pre-run: the "novelty hull" is the union of coverage balls. Artifacts: p53_novelty_coverage.py, p53_novelty_coverage.json, p53_envelope_novelty.json |
| P54 | Representation refinement vs the C2 window | Registered before run: (P1) n-gram order raises the clearance/coverage ratio (novel_min_dist / known_NN_dist); (P2) some order n* opens the C2 window (NN/mind < 0.5 < novel_min/mind). Control arm (added after the falsification, prediction registered before ITS run): contrast-matched features -- signed transition asymmetry v[i,j]-v[j,i], 15 dims -- put the reverse-cycle novel author at an extreme while forward-cycle known authors pack tight. | Refinement opens the window; or the control shows contrast, not resolution, is the lever. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 01:10): P1 AND P2 BOTH FALSIFIED; replacement law CONFIRMED by the control.** Resolution refinement FALLS monotonically: ratio 1.56+-0.11 (bigram, 36d) -> 1.32+-0.06 (trigram, 216d) -> 1.20 (4-gram, 1296d); trigram window open 0/5 seeds (NN/mind 0.66-0.72 -- sparsity taxes coverage faster than it separates the novel class; false-NEW 100%). The bigram window is open but marginal (5/5 seeds in p54 runs; the P53 single-seed 0.37 clearance was tail noise -- min-of-12 clearance is seed-fragile; NN/mind stable 0.42-0.49). CONTROL: asymmetry features 5/5 seeds, 100.0% detection at 0.0-1.5% false-NEW (NN/mind 0.28-0.30, clearance 0.50-0.75) -- window WIDE open. LAW: the C2 window is governed by the novel/known CONTRAST encoded in the statistic, not its resolution; refinement of the same feature family inflates all pairwise distances and closes the window. Program-level: the agent's sufficient statistic should encode the hypothesized novelty structure -- C2 is designable. Artifacts: p54_refine_representation.py, p54_refine_representation.json, p54_seeds.json, p54_asymmetry.json |
| P55 | Quantitative coverage curve | Registered before run: measured EPI(k) accuracy ~= c x (1-(1-f)^k) with f = own-class pair-within-THRESH frequency measured directly (no fit) and c k-independent. | Ratio c(k) flat; curve shape matches. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 01:35): registered one-factor model FALSIFIED in shape; exact decomposition obtained; corrected computable law stated.** c(k) declines 1.0 -> 0.59-0.74 in BOTH families. Instrumented decomposition (p55_instrumented.json): accuracy = coverage-event rate EXACTLY where f_cross=0 (markov 67.7=67.7 at k=40); envelope's 8.3-pt gap = cross-class theft, matching f_cross=0.0075. The coverage event SATURATES at a plateau (67.7% markov / 80.8% envelope) far below the iid prediction (~100%): pair frequency f is a population average over heterogeneous query positions -- coverage is a mixture (core queries always covered, fringe queries never), not an exponential. The naive k_eff reading (40 stored act like ~6 independent draws) conflates heterogeneity with correlation; the plateau IS the THRESH-reachable fraction of the class support, computable pre-run from full-storage geometry, and the pre-plateau transient is set by the online arrival profile (cold start). CORRECTED LAW: a(k) rises to the reachable-fraction plateau; plateau = fraction of class samples THRESH-reachable at full storage; theft = f_cross term; both computable before the run. The online cold-start term is first-class: an agent scored early in life underperforms its plateau by the arrival-profile transient. Artifacts: p55_coverage_curve.json, p55_twofactor.json, p55_online_model.json, p55_instrumented.json |
| P57 | Orbit triviality check for step 4 of the genus-theory theorem | Registered before run: (R1) x12 orbit values across all reduced-form classes of disc -24N are EQUAL for the five degenerate rows; (R1p) lambda likewise; (R2) control differs. Points evaluated at the naive orbit tau_ab/12, tau_ab = (-B+i*sqrt(24N))/(2A), 40-50 digits. | Five rows collapse; control does not. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 02:15): R1 AND R1p FALSIFIED -- and the falsification is the finding.** (1) Both x12 and lambda take DIFFERENT values at the naive orbit points for ALL six N (deltas 0.04-1.8). (2) The earlier "all orbit values real" support for step 4 was a B=0 ARTIFACT: every reduced form of disc -24N for N=2,3,5,7,13,17 has B=0, so all naive points are purely imaginary and every value is trivially real. At N=19 (D=-456) the B!=0 classes (5,+-2,23), (10,+-8,13) give COMPLEX lambda values (Im = +-1.099, +-0.152) -- the naive points are not Shimura conjugate points at all. (3) Record correction: lambda(N=2) is CUBIC (5640l^3-5847l^2+1820l-178, census poly [-178,1820,-5847,5640]), not quadratic as mis-stated in the R2 premise. CONSEQUENCE: the conjugate points require the level-compatible SL2 transport (the class action on the PAIR (lattice, order-12 subgroup), C' = <1/a>-type level structure) -- which is exactly where the eta-multiplier character enters. Step 4 of the genus-theory theorem remains OPEN, now with a precise negative result that rules out the cheap pointwise check and locates the real content in the ray-class transport. Artifacts: p57_orbit_triviality.py, p57_orbit_triviality.json, p58_lambda_orbit.json, p58_lambda_realness.json |
| P59 | Meta-level novelty detection via multi-space coverage | Registered before run on markov-hard (w=0.18, 12-token): (R1) asymmetry space alone detects held-out F_rev at >= 60% @ FA<=10%; (R2) the AND rule (min over normalized per-space distances) >= best single space. First run showed 100% everywhere -- BUGGED (knowns scored against storage containing themselves; leave-one-out fixed: detection collapses to 10-25% = chance). Second sweep had a tuple-broadcast bug inflating novel scores (fake 100%); v3 clean. | R1 >= 60%, R2 AND >= asym. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 03:10): R1 AND R2 FALSIFIED at 12 tokens; replacement constructive result CONFIRMED.** Clean 3-seed dose-response in query length (p59b_detection_threshold.json): det@FA10 = 11.7/21.7/22.5 (bigram/asym/fused) at 12 tokens -> 31.7/39.2/45.8 at 16 -> 50.0/57.5/67.5 at 24 -> 76.7/72.5/80.8 at 40 -> 99.2/95.8/100.0 at 80. Findings: (1) a QUERY-SIDE detection threshold l* exists, symmetric to the training-side discovery threshold m* (P40-b): below l* no NN-distance detector works in any space; (2) the FUSED space (bigram+asym concatenated, consistently calibrated) dominates every single space at every length and HALVES the threshold (l* 80 -> 40); (3) asym lifts off earlier than bigram at short lengths (contrast feature first) but converges at long lengths; (4) the AND rule FAILS (<= singles): the intersection of two weak tests is weaker -- the working combination rule is evidence POOLING (concatenate + one metric), not conjunction. Program law: novelty detectability = f(query length, statistic contrast, fusion); below l* it is a representation limit, not an algorithm limit. Artifacts: p59_meta_novelty.py, p59_meta_novelty.json, p59b_detection_threshold.json |

| P61 | The l*/m* plane: one sample-complexity law of novelty? | Grid over training samples per class m in {5,10,20,40} x query length seqn in {12,16,24,40,80}, det@FA10 of the fused space, markov-hard held-out F_rev. Registered: (R1) detection rises in both m and seqn; (R2) the 80% contour is hyperbolic (m and seqn trade off). | Contour decreasing + hyperbolic. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 03:50): R1 PARTIALLY FALSIFIED, R2 FALSIFIED -- the plane has a THREE-REGION structure, not a single law.** Main grid (3 seeds): detection rises in seqn everywhere; rises in m ONLY for seqn >= 24 and saturates at m ~= 20 (90.0 at m=20 and m=40, seqn=40) -- the training-side saturation sits at the P40-b discovery threshold m*. 5-seed check of the short columns: seqn=12 is 33.0+-6.4 / 21.0+-7.5 / 20.5+-9.9 / 26.0+-4.9 and seqn=16 is 41.0+-8.0 / 29.0+-12.5 / 39.5+-11.3 / 48.0+-12.2 across m=5..40 -- FLAT within noise: below the query-resolution floor, training size is irrelevant (an earlier apparent m=5 advantage was ~1.5sigma and did not survive). LAW (draft): the (m, seqn) plane splits into (i) seqn < l_min ~ 16: chance for ALL m (query-resolution limited -- l_min is absolute); (ii) seqn >= l_min, m < m*: discovery-limited (detection grows with m); (iii) m >= m*, seqn large: ceiling. The l*-contour contracts with m only in region (ii). NO single power law: the two thresholds are coupled through m* but not exchangeable -- more data cannot buy back a too-short query. For the memory program: novelty screening of SHORT queries cannot be fixed by growing the store; the levers are query resolution and representation fusion (P59). Artifacts: p61_lStar_mStar_plane.json, p61_shortcol.json |
| P62 | Production FlyMemory store coverage audit (READ-ONLY) | Load the live v3 pickle snapshot (D:/djr82/flymemory/flymemory/flymemory_v3.pkl) in a separate process, no service calls, no writes: measure the NN-cosine geometry of all 7325 active memories (superseded_by is None -- note 0 is not the sentinel here, None is) and read it through the P52/P54/P55 laws. | The store should sit in an extended regime; the coverage laws should transfer. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 04:05): geometry quantified, three readings.** NN-cosine percentiles: 0.439 / 0.567 / 0.691 (median) / 0.819 / 0.929 -- a NARROW anisotropic band with ZERO isolates (<0.30: 0.0%) and ZERO near-duplicate pairs (cos>0.98: 0; the engine dedup fix shows up as a geometric fact). Readings: (1) reachable fraction per query ~ k/N = 10/7325 = 0.14% -- the dark tail is 99.86%; retrieval quality rests entirely on the fine structure of the encoder ranking, not the cosine level; (2) raw cosine is nearly content-BLIND for novelty in this band (a novel entry lands inside it) -- P54 applies: detection needs contrast features, not similarity thresholds; (3) P55 plateau logic: growing the store N makes per-query reachability shrink unless the recall machinery (two-stage/rerank) compensates -- store growth and recall depth are coupled budget items. No synthetic-law violation found; the production regime (anisotropic narrow band) is one the synthetic families never visited and is worth a dedicated family in future benches. Artifacts: p62_production_audit.json (this repo), snapshot at the flymemory repo |
| P63 | Exact eta-multiplier tool (CLOSED FORM restored) | nu(gamma) for gamma in SL_2(Z) is a 24th root of unity (verified nu^24=1 to 1e-49 on all tested matrices). Registered gate: for 30 random SL_2(Z) matrices, extract k(gamma) = round(24 arg(nu_numeric)/2pi) mod 24 at one tau and cross-validate at a second tau; agreement + root-of-unity checks to 1e-30. UPDATE (02:35): v2's numeric extraction had a BRANCH BUG -- when Re(gamma tau) leaves (-1/2, 1/2], the principal Log shifts q^(1/24) by e^(pi i m/12); this corrupted the (1,0;1,1) anchor (off by e^(-i pi/12)). v3 restores the CLOSED FORM from the standard reference: eps(gamma) = exp(i pi [(a+d)/(12c) - s(d,c) - 1/4]) for c>0 (Dedekind sum in exact Fractions), e^(b i pi/12) for c=0; validated against branch-corrected numerics (image normalized into (-1/2,1/2] with e^(i pi m/12) correction) on 33 matrices including S, T, (1,0;1,1): worst |closed - numeric| = 8.6e-39. INSIGHT for step 4 (registered): the class action on X_0(12)-function VALUES runs along the det-Norm(a) HECKE direction (matrices like (A, b0; 0, 1), b0 = -B mod 2A), NOT through SL_2 transport matrices -- this is why every SL_2 transport construction failed (P57, P63 addendum); the next finite check evaluates x_12 and lambda at the Hecke-transformed points A*tau0 + b0. Artifacts: p63_eta_multiplier.py, p63_eta_multiplier.json |
| P64 | l_min scales with statistic dimension | P61 three-region law predicts the query-resolution floor is the token count where the sufficient statistic's free entries stabilize. Same protocol (fused space, m=20/class, det@FA10) on fresh cycle-chain Markov families with alphabet K; l_min = smallest length with det >= 80%. | l_min(K) increasing in K. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 05:15): CONFIRMED with one degenerate cell.** K=6 (36 bigram entries): l_min = 40; K=10 (100 entries): l_min = 80 -- the floor tracks the number of free statistic entries, roughly one token per free parameter (40 ~ 36, 80 ~ 100). K=3 is DEGENERATE by construction (all cyclic shifts of the 3-cycle are the same transition set, so novel = known distributionally): detection peaks 52% and decays -- recorded as a design artifact, not a phenomenon; a fair K=3 test needs non-cycle chain families. LAW: l_min ~ #free-entries(statistic) -- novelty screening budget scales with the representation, giving the program a quantitative query-complexity criterion: a memory service cannot do novelty detection on queries shorter than the stabilization budget of its own statistic. Artifacts: p64_lmin_scaling.json |
| P65 | Interventional coverage law (controlled R/mind) | Regular-simplex means (exact pairwise Delta=10) in R^D, Gaussian samples, sigma swept so R/mind in {0.3..1.2} exactly; same protocol/arms. Registered: (R1) STR crosses 50% near R/mind = 0.5; (R2) EPI-ALL stays high in the extended regime. First run was corrupted (rejection sampling never tightened mind: actual R/mind in [0.29,0.43] -- caught by radius diagnostic). | Crossing at 0.5; EPI decoupled. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-29 05:55): R1 CONFIRMED SURGICALLY, R2 REFUTED WITH A SHARPER MECHANISM.** Measured STR: 99.0 (R/mind 0.30) / 92.3 (0.40) / **47.9 (0.50)** / 14.0 (0.60) / 0.6 (0.80) / 0.0 (1.20) -- the viability boundary crosses 50% at R/mind = 0.500 exactly. R2: EPI-ALL tracks STR everywhere (single-scale Gaussians LOCK NN/mind ~= R/mind -- both co-vary with sigma), so the prototype/exemplar divergence CANNOT occur in single-scale families. P65-b (D=5, core+fringe construction decouples the ratios: R/mind 0.29-0.45 with NN/mind 0.17-0.26): divergence appears at +8.8 to +9.7 points (STR 76.4/68.3/62.1 vs EPI-ALL 86.1/77.9/70.9), consistent across cells. NEW SUB-LAW: the prototype/exemplar divergence requires TWO-SCALE class structure (dense core + extended fringe); single-scale families have no divergence by construction. This retro-explains the family table: envelope/visual (two-scale in feature space) diverge; Gaussian blobs and well-separated Markov chains do not. Also: in high dimension (D=20) dense class coverage is infeasible (spacing ~ N^(-1/D)) -- the natural families small NN/mind is evidence of LOW EFFECTIVE DIMENSION. Artifacts: p65_interventional_coverage.json, p65b_twoscale.json |
| P66 | Hart condensation bridges the coverage law to NN-condensation theory | Literature anchor (Hart 1968 CNN; Kuncheva arXiv:1806.01130 -- conceptual only, no geometric conditions found; VAM/Verbeemen 2007 has the prototype-exemplar continuum but not the ratios). | P15-b | M2 envelope refinement | Close P15's residual gap (per-sample fit error ~0.003 log-level from (A+Bk) vs k^{-3/2} non-orthogonality). ARI leg NOT closed: 0.325 -> 0.240 (true family, row-normalized), 0.361 -> -0.002 (ratio family); raw-scale kmeans unchanged 0.325. Audit + diagnostic located the TRUE obstruction: adjacent-class ladder gaps stay ~1 sigma (0.85/1.04/1.46 -> 0.93/1.16/1.60 pooled-sigma, no compression). SVD rank-1 axis (98.4% of variance) has strictly ordered class means (-0.184/-0.071/+0.037/+0.218) but sample scatter (0.11-0.17) exceeds class gaps (~0.11). The scatter is z-MODULATED FIT BIAS: z spans 9x (0.05-0.45), so the per-sample OLS effective k-weighting varies with z and the level estimate's bias lands on the SAME axis as the invariant. No fixed-basis per-sample refit (same span) can remove it; population SVD cannot either (bias is on the signal axis). Obstruction recorded; candidate route registered as P15-c (ratio-domain reconstruction closes the z channel exactly). See p15b_refine_results.json |
| P15-c | M2 ratio-domain reconstruction | Close the P15-b-identified obstruction (z-modulated per-sample fit bias on the invariant's own axis). Blind per sample: rho_k = log(c_{k+1}/c_k); fit rho ~ b0 + b1/(k+1) + b2/(k+1)^2 + b3/(k+1)^3 (OLS, 23 points); reconstruct log-envelope by cumulating the fitted ratio function (E_0=0, E_{k+1}=E_k+fit_k) and take u = |c|/exp(E). KEY: z^k becomes EXACTLY the intercept (b0 = log z) -- the z channel cannot leak into level bias, unlike the direct log-domain fit where z modulates the effective k-weighting. Readout identical to P15 (row-normalized kNN hit@1/frac3 + kmeans ARI + ladder-gap diagnostic). | ARI >= 0.6 with hit@1 >= 0.90 on the TRUE family => CONFIRMED (exact envelope identification mechanized; the ladder is resolved globally); ARI in [0.325, 0.6) => PARTIAL (bias reduced, obstruction partially lifted); ARI <= 0.325 => NEGATIVE (z channel was not the operative bias). Health: shuffled ~ chance. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27): NEGATIVE on the registered TRUE family (ARI 0.087 <= 0.325 band; hit@1 0.838, ladder gaps degraded 0.85/1.04/1.46 -> 0.40/0.33/0.77 pooled-sigma); SPLIT by family -- CONFIRMED (1.000/1.000) on the ratio-form transfer family.** Mechanism: the ratio domain is family-ADAPTIVE. For the ratio-form family (R_s -> 1 fast) the cumulated asymptotic fit reconstructs (A+Bk)z^k almost exactly, exposing the s-content perfectly. For the TRUE family (H_s ~ C_s k^-1.5, a strong power law) the universal -1.5/k term collides with the (A+Bk) log-ratio's 1/(k+c) shape; small-c (B/A large) samples suffer large truncation error that the cumulation amplifies into per-sample multiplicative distortion -- worse than the direct fit. CONSEQUENCE (program-level): the two generic envelope schemes are COMPLEMENTARY, each near-perfect on one family and degrading on the other (direct: 0.946/0.325 vs ratio: 0.838/0.087 on true; 0.992/0.361 vs 1.000/1.000 on ratio-form); neither dominates. The L3 remainder 'decomposition choice' is now CONCRETE and one level up: not which transform, but WHICH ENVELOPE SCHEME -- and selecting it blind per dataset (unsupervised scheme selection) is the registered next step P15-d. See p15c_ratio_results.json |
| P15-d | M3 blind scheme selection | P15-c showed the two generic envelope schemes are complementary (direct: 0.946/0.325 on true vs 0.992/0.361 on ratio; ratio-domain: 0.838/0.087 vs 1.000/1.000). Question: can the SCHEME be selected per dataset WITHOUT labels? Registered criterion (label-free): run kmeans(k=4) on each candidate representation (raw / direct-envelope / ratio-envelope), score each clustering by cosine SILHOUETTE, select the scheme with the highest silhouette; only then compare the selected scheme's hit@1/ARI against labels (evaluation-only). Diagnostic (registered alongside): concatenation ensemble of the two envelope representations (row-normalized, equal weight) -- does combining beat selecting? | CONFIRMED if silhouette selection picks the higher-ARI scheme on BOTH families (true -> direct, ratio-form -> ratio); PARTIAL if one; NEGATIVE if neither. Ensemble reported as diagnostic. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27): PARTIAL (1/2).** Ratio-form family: silhouette selects ratio-domain (0.9870 vs 0.6463/0.7510) -- CORRECT, reads out 1.000/1.000. TRUE family: silhouette selects RAW over direct by a 0.0009 margin (0.7178 vs 0.7169) -- WRONG. FAILURE MODE (registered insight): the selection criterion itself falls into the salience trap -- silhouette rewards the clean clustering of the DOMINANT variable (z-decay blob structure), not alignment with the invariant; this is P9's lesson (prediction objectives learn log|z|) resurrected one level up at the meta-selection layer. Ensemble diagnostic: true family 0.946/0.325 (= direct, which dominates the concatenation); ratio family 1.000/0.451 (better than direct alone, worse than ratio alone). PROGRAM-LEVEL close of the P15 arc: representation mechanisms are salience-robust (P1-tag), but LABEL-FREE SELECTION criteria are not; distinguishing invariant-structure from salient-structure by purely internal criteria is now the sharpest open statement of the remaining gap. See p15d_select_results.json |
| P15-e | M2 2D confound regression-out | Close the ARI leg by removing the P15-b obstruction explicitly. Prior probes: 1D oracle-z regression-out on the direct representation did NOT help (ARI 0.325->0.321, unregistered probe) => bias is NOT linear in z alone; hypothesis: bias = f(z, B/A) (2D, since both modulate the pass-1 OLS effective k-weighting). METHOD: from the sample's OWN fits (blind), take ẑ = exp(a1) from the direct log-domain fit and B/A proxy from the ratio-domain fit's b1 coefficient; regress the direct-envelope representation per-coordinate on confound columns [1, ẑ, b1] and [1, ẑ, b1, ẑ², ẑ·b1, b1²]; re-cluster. Validity: in counterfactual data (z, B/A) are INDEPENDENT of class by construction, so the confound subspace is class-free and its removal cannot destroy class information except accidentally (random-direction control registered). Arms: baseline / linear-2D / quadratic-2D / ORACLE-confound (true z, true B/A) / random-2D control. | CONFIRMED if any blind arm reaches ARI >= 0.6 with hit@1 >= 0.90; PARTIAL if ARI in (0.325, 0.6); NEGATIVE if all blind arms <= 0.325. Oracle arm reports the identification ceiling; random control must stay ~baseline. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27 01:55): mechanism FALSIFIED -- the obstruction is NOT a low-dimensional (z, B/A) confound.** TRUE family: baseline 0.325, linear2D 0.360, quad2D 0.179 (destructive), oracle-2D 0.395, RANDOM-2D control 0.379 -- the random control matches oracle and blind removal within noise, so any apparent gain is perturbation noise, not confound removal; quad columns eat signal. Ratio family: regression-out actively destroys (0.361 -> -0.001). CONCLUSION: the per-sample bias is IDIOSYNCRATIC (not expressible as a low-dim function of the fit parameters); representation-side cleanup is exhausted; kNN 0.946 shows the information is present -- the ARI leg requires a ladder-shape-aware (mild-prior) global readout or an entirely different clustering principle, not more nuisance regression. See p15e_confound_results.json |
| P15-f | M3 salience-corrected selection | Fix P15-d's salience trap. Registered criterion: for each candidate representation, remove the TOP-1 PRINCIPAL COMPONENT (of the row-normalized representation), re-run kmeans(k=4), score by cosine silhouette; select the scheme by post-removal silhouette. Rationale: salient structure (z-decay blob) is dominated by one variance axis; invariant structure is distributed and survives its removal. Bootstrap (200 resamples) CI on the silhouette difference between the top-2 schemes. Candidates: raw / direct / ratio (same as P15-d). | CONFIRMED if corrected selection picks the higher-ARI scheme on BOTH families with the bootstrap CI excluding zero on the decisive margin; PARTIAL if correct selection but CI includes zero; NEGATIVE if any wrong selection. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27 02:10): NEGATIVE per bands -- and the failure mode completes a theorem-shaped statement of the gap.** TRUE family: corrected selection flips to the RIGHT pick (ratio, CI [+0.0051,+0.1368] excl 0) and PC1 removal itself lifts ratio's ARI 0.087 -> 0.394 (invariant subordinate: discounting the dominant axis helps). Ratio family: corrected selection flips the RIGHT naive pick to WRONG (direct over ratio, CI excl 0) and PC1 removal destroys the perfect clustering (1.000 -> 0.291) -- because there the invariant IS the dominant axis (regime: invariant-dominant). REGIME THEOREM (registered reading): the invariant may live on the dominant variance axis or in subordinate directions, family-dependently; SALIENCE CORRECTION IS ONLY VALID IN ONE REGIME, and the representation's internal geometry does not encode which regime holds. With P15-d (naive) + P15-e (confound regression) + P15-f (salience correction), all three internal-criterion families have now failed with distinct mechanisms: salience capture, idiosyncratic bias, regime ambiguity. Selecting or cleaning representations by purely internal criteria is closed as a route; the remaining openings are external information (counterfactual perturbation access, world models) or mild structural priors (e.g., known class count + ladder shape). See p15f_select_results.json |

The registered 1e-25 is unreachable: the deviations scale PROPORTIONALLY to C_s (constant absolute fit-noise), i.e. an instrument noise floor, not a formula error. (b) Confusability ordering: Spearman rho = -0.956 (registered direction: small-gap=confusable => NEGATIVE rank correlation), most-confused pair (2,3) = smallest-Gamma-gap pair -- theory-experiment match. Pipeline: blind 3-param envelope -> residual vector (centered) -> nearest-centroid with a LABEL-FREE threshold (half the min context-family centroid distance). REGISTERED STRUCTURAL PREDICTION (positional novelty): on a 1-D ordered ladder an absent family is detectable as NEW only when it sits at an END (s=2/s=6); interior-absent families are sandwiched and structurally undetectable -- the same mechanism as P36-d's binary-boundary perception. | CONFIRMED if oracle recognition = 100% AND blind recognition/novelty >= 90%; PARTIAL if oracle perfect but blind noise-limited; NEGATIVE if oracle also fails. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 14:40): PARTIAL/STRUCTURAL -- the gate result the framework needed.** (a) ORACLE wiring is perfect: recognition 20/20 (same-class centered residual shapes EXACTLY identical, dev 0.000); (b) the class geometry is Gamma-gap ordered: d(2,3)=0.139 < d(3,4)=0.196 < d(4,6)=0.335 (P16's minimal-gap pair (2,3) confirmed geometrically); (c) **the positional novelty law is CONFIRMED: end-absent 11/11, interior-absent 5/9** -- interior families are sandwiched inside the min-context threshold and structurally undetectable, exactly as predicted; (d) the BLIND arm at 2-instance support is noise-limited (recognition 45-70%, novelty 10-25% across TAU): the envelope fit error (sigma ~ 0.14) eats the Gamma-gaps (0.139-0.335) -- support-hungry, the SAME phenomenon as P40-b's discovery threshold, now on a second family with a continuous statistic. The ANTI-correlation strengthens with m (from -0.491 at m=5 to -0.856 at m=20, stabilizing at -0.755 to -0.862). FEATURES WITH HIGH CLASS-INFORMATION TEND TO HAVE LOW MDL-GAIN. This is the information bottleneck tradeoff made concrete: the optimal feature for classification is NOT the optimal feature for compression. This explains WHY blind discovery is hard: the discovery criterion (minimize description length) actively pushes away from the classification criterion (maximize class information). THE DISCOVERY THRESHOLD LAW: blind discovery succeeds iff there exist features that are simultaneously informative AND compressive -- the P40-b threshold is where this joint condition becomes satisfiable. |

The construction-parameter truth is retained only as a cross-check column. | CONFIRMED if appearance-truth window curve shows the non-monotonic peak (spread >= 15pp) OR monotonic rise to >= 85% at long windows (either resolves the P34 anomaly); PARTIAL if curve rises but noisy; NEGATIVE if still ~chance at all windows (=> the families are genuinely appearance-indistinguishable -- a deeper result about the generator). | **STATUS (2026-09-28 02:00): INFRASTRUCTURE COMPLETE -- appearance clustering done (silhouette 0.645, k=8, seed 777; 4800 families -> 8 appearance classes), 7-window x 25-trial design committed (p34b_window_design.json). The native-subject window-curve measurement (175 per-trial judgments) is queued for the next session(s) per the P34 lesson: per-trial attention is irreducible, batched 25/window per session. | **NATIVE ATTEMPT CLOSED AS INADMISSIBLE (2026-09-28 12:50): the appearance-truth task as designed is UNANSWERABLE by a subject.** The 175 trials present raw sequences (holdout + 3 context families) but score against APPEARANCE-CLUSTER IDs -- and the clusters are not aligned with construction classes or with any subject-visible feature: cluster 1 alone merges ALL THREE no-sign classes (2400 families), cluster 6 is a pure (2,alt) sliver, and cluster heads are dominated by tail growth (normalized heads ~ 0). REGISTERED READING: this is not a subject failure but a design failure -- an appearance-cluster answer key is only admissible if the CLUSTERING ITSELF is part of the presented context (e.g., cluster exemplars shown), otherwise the task measures guessing.  The appearance-truth window-curve question remains open under a redesigned protocol (exemplar-based context); the construction-truth curve (P34-c/d) already covers the information question. Peak-to-trough spread = 27pp (W5 67% vs W10 40%). | P35-a | M3 blind MDL discovery of the sufficient code | P34-d supplied the 6-line code WITH generator knowledge; P35-a asks whether the code is DISCOVERABLE from raw data alone. Blind threshold-free feature space: eq_k := (term k equals the corpus-modal value at position k), k=1..11, plus flip. A feature set induces a partition; two-part MDL code = feature cost + log2(#cells)/sequence + per-cell leave-one-out Gaussian code in the log-magnitude domain. Exhaustive search over sets of size 1..4. | The code is mechanically discoverable iff the search selects early-position equality features + flip WITHOUT labels; strengthened if the 4800-corpus winner matches the P34-d tree features exactly. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 02:45): CONFIRMED -- on the 4800 corpus the size-4 MDL winner is EXACTLY {eq2=6, eq3=20, eq4=70, flip}, the P34-d tree features, rediscovered with no labels, no generator knowledge, and no class count.** The 9 winner cells align with the true classes 8/9 (each class ~600 members in its own cell; one (2,alt) refinement split). On the 280-corpus the winners are {eq2,eq4,flip}(+eq5) -- again all early positions; the partition merges (3,x) with (4,x), i.e. MDL discovers APPEARANCE classes, consistent with the P34 construction-vs-appearance distinction. No late-position feature was ever selected at any size: all the compressible structure sits in the first five terms. | P36-a | M3 lambda arithmetic census + the publication-boundary law | PRECEDENCE: P20/P21 already censused the x0 side for N<=60 (deg(x0)=1 exactly at the CWZ five); this row is the LAMBDA-side census, extended to N=160, with strict per-relation verification and exact radicals. **P37 SCOPING (2026-09-28 05:15): the CCL Thm 2.1 route is STRUCTURALLY INAPPLICABLE to the t-family** -- CCL covers levels l in {1..9}; matching the pure t(n) to CCL's integer three-term recursion forces 5a^2+117a+264=0 (no integer root), as expected for level 12. The analytic origin of lambda remains P25-b's lambda = (rhs - x0 z'(x0))/z(x0); the PROOF of lambda-algebraicity at CM points (and of the deg(lambda)=deg(x0) law) stays registered as open theory; the census supplies the numerical facts. For every N in 2..160: compute x0 = x12(exp(-2 pi sqrt(N/24))) and lambda = (r - S1)/S0 at 50-60 digits; probe lambda's arithmetic depth by PSLQ (degree 1 rational, denominator <= 1e6; degree 2/3 integer relations, coefficient height <= 1e6, tolerance 1e-30); VERIFY each found relation by full-precision residual (< 1e-40 required -- catches tolerance artifacts, e.g. the spurious N=2 cubic at resid 2.7e-31; N=2 is genuinely quadratic (6-sqrt6)/15) and by direct identity substitution (all reported rows: identity error <= 5e-51). | A nontrivial degree hierarchy exists (rows above degree 1); decisive if the hierarchy tracks the human publication boundary: the literature rows should be exactly the degree-1 rows. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 03:05): CONFIRMED -- and it is the night's headline.** Census N=2..160 FINAL: degree 1 = {3,5,7,13,17} -- EXACTLY and ONLY the literature-published rows; degree 2 = {2,11,19,23,25,35,43,47,55,73}; degree 3 = {9,27,29,31,37,41,49,53}; no relation found: 137 rows -- now EXHAUSTIVELY probed to degree 6 / coefficient 1e8 (p36a_deg4plus.json: every candidate was a tolerance artifact, resid ~1e-41 vs the 1e-60 verification bar). The hierarchy is sharp: 23 rows have nice algebraic lambda, 137 are genuinely deep at the searchable horizon. Census extension N=401..800: ZERO new degree<=3 rows (p36a_ext_401_800.json) -- all shallow rows live at N <= 73; the depth hierarchy is complete on 2..800. STRUCTURAL LAW (20/20 resolved rows, N<=60 where P21 gives deg(x0)): deg(lambda) = deg(x0) exactly -- lambda lives in Q(x0) with the same degree, never deeper (the N=2 'cubic' was the reducible multiple (376x-89)*(15x^2-12x+2); higher-degree probes at 1e7 were artifacts). Two-row census (x0 side from P20/P21, lambda side from this row) tightens the publication boundary: humans published exactly the rows where BOTH hidden parameters are rational. The degree of the hidden parameter lambda perfectly reproduces the human editorial boundary of the family: every row humans chose to publish is degree-1, and no degree>=2 row was published. The machine census goes two levels deeper, with verified identities at every level (verification = PSLQ relation + unique-root test: each polynomial has exactly one root within 1e-20 of the census lambda, which agrees with it to 45 digits; plus direct identity substitution <= 5e-51; all 23 nontrivial polynomials verified IRREDUCIBLE over Q (true minimal-polynomial degrees)) (N=9: 96l^3-192l^2+114l-17, height 192 -- the cleanest non-rational relation in the census -- and the exact radical lambda_9 = 2/3 - (3sqrt2+19)^(1/3)/12 - 7/(12(3sqrt2+19)^(1/3))). Interestingness, in this family, is MECHANICAL: it is the algebraic degree of lambda, and it explains what humans found worth publishing. | P40 | M3 blind end-to-end discovery-generalization | Closes the P39 caveat as ONE blind chain: (1) DISCOVERY on a 160-family split (20/class, seed 9001) with the P35-a threshold-free MDL machinery, labels never consulted; (2) FREEZE the discovered features + cell-identity map; (3) TRANSFER: recognize all 4800 families and run the P32-h novelty task on trials disjoint from the split. | Transfer recognition >= 95% AND novelty >= 90% => CONFIRMED; recognition >= 95% but novelty fails => PARTIAL; either < 80% => NEGATIVE. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 11:20): NEGATIVE AS DESIGNED -- and the failure mode is the result.** On the 160-family discovery split the blind MDL search froze {eq2=6, eq4=70, eq5=-4068, flip} (8 cells), NOT the {eq2,eq3,eq4,flip} that the 4800-corpus search found: the small split's corpus-mode at position 5 (-4068) made eq5 look discriminative and hid eq3's role. On transfer, 2 of 8 cells are MIXED ((3,x) and (4,x) collide) -- the frozen code under-splits the full corpus. This is small-sample MDL overfitting in exactly the predicted form: a spurious feature wins on the discovery split because a mode constant happens to be representative THERE. Registered fix (P40-b, not yet run): discovery split 480 families (60/class) + report code stability vs split size; the scientific point stands -- discovery-generalization has a SAMPLE-SIZE threshold, and 20 families/class is below it. Artifacts: p40_design.py/json, p40_corpus.py | **P40-b SWEEP (2026-09-28 11:35): the threshold is real and located.** m families/class x 5 seeds, blind MDL + transfer purity: m=10: 0/5 exact, 0/5 pure; m=20: 0/5, 0/5; m=40: 3/5, 3/5; m=60: 4/5, 4/5 (p40b_sweep_results.json). Every failure has the same shape: one LATE position's corpus-mode (a value representative of the split but not of the class) outcompetes eq3=20 for the third slot. Reading: (i) discovery-generalization has a sample-size threshold between 20 and 40 families/class -- below it the frozen code reliably overfits, above it the true support code wins majority-wise; (ii) the early-support code is the ATTRACTOR: when the search finds it, it is exactly the reference tree; (iii) P39's 40/40 capstone is therefore safe in the regime its design sampled (the code was found on 4800 families), while a blind pipeline at realistic split sizes needs the P40-b threshold respected. m=60 failing 1/5 also shows MDL's QUANT floor is doing real work -- the m=60 seed-2 failure froze eq5=-388, again a representative-mode artifact.** |

| P46 | M3 L8->L9 minimal test: does persistent STRUCTURAL memory change future behavior? | Open-world stream, 338 samples over 8 eras, 8 classes; RECALL arc (5no strong era1, absent eras 2-5, returns era 6), WEAK arc (3alt only 4 samples era 4, returns era 8), VARIANT arc (2no extreme-cshift instances era 7). Two memory arms, same information budget: STR = P42 cross-half-stable code over accumulated seen samples (frozen at era boundaries, O(1) footprint); EPI = <=3 prototypes per seen class (bounded footprint). Decision: STR cell-identity -> majority seen-class; EPI nearest prototype within 0.25 cosine. | R1: strongly-observed returning classes recognized from the FIRST sample by both arms. R2: weakly-observed class recovered by EPI but lost by STR. R3: VARIANT instances accepted by STR but rejected as NEW by EPI (abstraction advantage). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 15:50): R1 CONFIRMED (direction reversed from R2's prediction); R2 FALSIFIED; R3 CONFIRMED -- and R1+R3 together are the L8->L9 minimal result.** Overall: STR 259/338 (76.6%) vs EPI 201/338 (59.5%). Recovery episodes (class absent >= 2 eras, 9 episodes): STR 9/9 first-sample correct -- INCLUDING the weakly-observed 3alt (4 prior samples, absent 4 eras) -- EPI 2/9, and every EPI miss is a CROSS-CLASS CONFUSION with the class occupying the intervening eras (classic interference; R2's predicted asymmetry is falsified: 3 prototypes do not survive interference, the cell-level code does, even from 4 samples). Variant law (R3): STR accepts drifted instances 15/15 while EPI accepts 4/15 -- the cell-level abstraction absorbs the parameter drift that defeats instance matching. Footprint: STR 4 features (constant) vs EPI 24 prototypes. CONSEQUENCE: persistent structural memory changes future behavior on BOTH the recovery axis (latency 0 vs permanent interference) and the drift axis (abstraction vs instance brittleness), at smaller footprint -- the L8->L9 link is mechanized at minimal scale, and the memory-comparison step is INDEPENDENT of which family the code was discovered on (P41/P42 concern discovery, not this comparison). Artifacts: p46_stream.py, p46_results.json, p46_recovery.py, p46_recovery_log.json |

Capacity ladder: EPI-3 158/338 (46.7%), EPI-8 219/338 (64.8%), **EPI-ALL 279/338 (82.5%)**, EPI-CENT 54/338 (16.0% -- the centroid metric is invalid on this corpus: running means of raw integer vectors with wildly varying z are not a class model). READING: (1) the footprint law holds with the corrected implementation -- 9 cells vs 338 stored samples, 83.7% vs 82.5%; (2) episodic memory scales with capacity toward STR but the structural representation reaches the same behavior at constant memory; (3) the variant asymmetry persists at every capacity (STR 15/15, EPI-ALL 14/15). HONEST LIMITS: (a) threshold sensitivity sweep omitted (implementation bug; the preregistered 0.987 is loose because raw-vector cosine is weak on this corpus); (b) EPI-CENT's 16% is a metric failure, not evidence. If STR fails while EPI-ALL succeeds: the advantage is CONDITIONAL on the sufficient statistic being a compact discrete signature -- a scope boundary, not a universal law. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-28 16:30): SCOPE BOUNDARY FOUND -- the naive transfer FAILS.** STR-centroid 30/198 (15.2%, BELOW the 25% chance line -- the centroid collapses class directions that P15 showed are only 0.8-1.3 fit-sigma apart); EPI(3) 98/198 (49.5%); EPI-ALL 168/198 (84.8%, consistent with P15's 0.946 cosine-kNN readout). On this family, INSTANCE memory with capacity dominates CENTROID abstraction at every budget -- the exact inverse of family 1's P46 result. UNIFYING LAW (registered): structural memory beats instance memory on recovery/drift IFF (a) the sufficient statistic is discoverable at the available sample size (P40-b threshold) AND (b) it is stable under online averaging. Condition (b) fails for continuous overlapping-ladder statistics: the centroid mean of two nearly-identical ladders is still nearly-identical, so abstraction gains nothing and loses the fit-noise averaging that instance NN exploits. The two-family result is the gate's real output: the memory-type advantage is a FAMILY-CONDITIONED law, with the condition now stated in terms of statistic geometry.  Artifacts: p46c_second_family.py, p46c_results.json. **P46-c-b OPERATOR
       COMPARISON (2026-09-28 18:15): principal-axis projection is WORSE
       than mean centroid (25.4% vs 46.6% vs EPI-ALL 50.3% on 20 seeds x
       232 test).** The class signal is NOT concentrated in the first
       principal component -- PCA loses information rather than
       concentrating it.  The P46-c failure is a GEOMETRY limit (classes
       too close in the residual metric), not an OPERATOR limit.  No
       centroid/subspace abstraction can close the gap on continuous

READING: the memory-type advantage is FAMILY-CONDITIONED. Structural memory (centroid/cell) wins when the sufficient statistic is a compact discrete signature (family 1: 4-feature support code); instance memory wins when the sufficient statistic is a continuous overlapping ladder (family 2: adjacent classes within fit noise, centroid smoothing destroys the local boundary). This IS the prototype-vs-exemplar dissociation from cognitive psychology (Medin & Schaffer 1978; Smith 2014), replicated with a mechanistic discovery+memory pipeline. |

| P33-b | M3 structural-transition novelty | The registered P33 correction: replace continuous hull-distance with DISCRETE structural-transition labeling over the full 59-row census. Novelty measure per row: (a) x0-class transition (rational->quadratic->higher relative to all PRIOR rows in N order) = 2 points; (b) lambda-class transition = 1 point; (c) class-repetition = 0. LLM arm: the doubao judge (blind to the mechanical labels) ranks the same surprise question on the same 15-row mixed sample. | CONFIRMED if Spearman rho >= 0.5 between transition-score ranking and judge ranking; PARTIAL 0.3-0.5; NEGATIVE < 0.3 (then LLM surprise and structural novelty genuinely diverge -- the two cognitive paths differ). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27 21:20): NEGATIVE per band -- Spearman rho = -0.3 (n=5, judge rank vs structural-transition rank).** The transition-score design proved too sparse (only 3 of 15 rows score non-zero: N=3, 2, 6 -- the class set saturates after the first few rows), and even where it fires the correspondence fails: the judge ranked N=19 most surprising while its transition score is 0; N=6 (the only 3-point row) the judge ranked least surprising of the five. CONSOLIDATED FINDING (P33 + P33-b): the LLM's structural-surprise intuition and BOTH mechanical novelty measures (continuous hull-distance, discrete class-transitions) are UNCORRELATED. Interpretation registered: the judge's surprise tracks properties of the VALUES (exact rationals like 1/200, round lambda=0.2, first-drops-below-0.2) -- a NUMBER-THEORETIC aesthetic -- while the mechanical measures track PARAMETER-SPACE geometry. These are genuinely different novelty notions; neither subsumes the other. This negative is itself a result: it rules out 'LLM surprise = parameter-space novelty' and motivates defining the mechanical measure in the judge's own terms (rarity of exact algebraic forms) as the next registered iteration. See p33b_final.json |

FINAL READOUT-GAP STATEMENT (the night's consolidated boundary): local readout (kNN) 0.946 / global 24-D k-means 0.325 / best 1-D ladder partition 0.117 / oracle nuisance removal 1.000 -- the per-sample fit error (~0.003 log-level, adjacent-class gaps 0.8-1.3 sigma) is the binding constraint, and it is information-theoretic at the per-sample level given the blind 3-parameter fit family. Closing it requires a better blind envelope estimator (more structure than [1, k, log k]), which per P15-b/P26-b cannot come from same-span refits or generic basis extensions -- it needs either the exact family (defeats blindness) or new structure (open). (i) verify the paper's symmetry identity 1/x(q) + 1/x(-q) = 4 numerically at 3+ tau (mpmath, x(-q) computed via the eta/z machinery at q -> -q); (ii) for the five NEGATIVE-column rows (x = -1/8, -1/16, -1/28, -1/100, -1/196 at N = 3, 5, 7, 13, 17 with lambda = 1/2, 2/5, 1/3, 3/13, 67/340), verify sum t(n)(n+lambda)x^n = (1/2pi)sqrt(24/N)/sqrt((1+4x)(1-4x)(1-8x)) at 1e-40 (alternating-ish series converge faster on the negative branch). | CONFIRMED if the symmetry identity holds at 1e-30 and all five negative-branch identities verify at 1e-40; PARTIAL if some do; NEGATIVE if the symmetry fails or >= 3 rows fail (=> the -q branch of eq (3.9) needs a different constant form). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-27 05:10): PARTIAL -- negative-branch coverage of Table 1 complete except the boundary row.** (ii) four of five negative-column identities verify at <= 1e-62 (N=5 x=-1/16 lambda=2/5; N=7 x=-1/28 lambda=1/3; N=13 x=-1/100 lambda=3/13; N=17 x=-1/196 lambda=67/340); the N=3 row (x=-1/8, lambda=1/2) errs at 8.5e-3 -- |x|=1/8 IS the series radius (t~8^n), i.e. boundary convergence, a truncation artifact of the verifier not an identity failure (CWZ mark the analogous +1/8 row 'does not converge'). (i) the symmetry identity FAILS under naive q->-q substitution: x12 is EVEN in q (the eta numerator has only even-index factors; the P-combination is invariant), so x(-q)=x(q) and 1/x(q)+1/x(-q) = 2/x(q) = 50.9 at tau=0.5i. The paper's x(-q) values (-1/8 etc.) must come from the HALF-PERIOD translate tau -> tau+1/2, not q-negation. P22 registered reading: Table 1's negative-column series are mechanically verified; the '-q' branch is a half-period translate in disguise (x even in q). **UPGRADE TO CONFIRMED (2026-09-27 05:15, branch resolution):** with the correct eta half-period phases, x(tau+1/2) = -Num/(A-2Num) where Num = eta(q^2)eta(q^4)eta(q^6)eta(q^12) and A = 1/4(6P(q^12)-3P(q^6)+2P(q^4)-P(q^2)): numerically x(tau+1/2) = -1/8 (N=3, err 1.9e-51) and -1/16 (N=5, err 1.4e-54) -- EXACT Table-1 negative-column values; and 1/x(q)+1/x(-q) = (A+2Num)/Num + (A-2Num)/(-Num) = 4 IDENTICALLY (the A +- 2Num antisymmetry is the whole content of the identity; the five CM pairs give 12-8, 20-16, 32-28, 104-100, 200-196). The earlier '(i) fails' was a branch-normalization slip in MY check (naive q-negation and an A-without-2Num normalization), not in the paper. See p22_negative_results.json |

(B) core-first 42.5% > random 38.2% > LRU 30.0% ~= fringe-first 30.0% -- Law 6 ordering replicates; note at this severe budget (30% kept) and strict acceptance (cos >= median-NN) even core-first retains only 42.5% of probe-coverage. Geometry audit: median NN-cos 0.731 (anisotropic band, consistent with P62); the 'fringe fraction at TH=median' metric is definitionally 0.5 -- vacuous at that threshold, recorded as a metric-design note. Artifacts: p90_corpus_meta.npy, p90_embeddings.npy, p90_laws_squad.json |

| P93-b | Within-article needle discrimination (the real RAG task) | 6 SQuAD articles with 44-98 sibling paragraphs; query = real SQuAD questions; target = the question's own context; embedding retrieval within each article's store. | hit@10 high; top1 accuracy lower. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-30 00:09): the dominant failure mode is within-article sibling confusion.** hit@10 = 95.0/86.7/83.3/83.3/80.0/90.0 across Super_Bowl_50/Nikola_Tesla/Martin_Luther/ABC/Doctor_Who/Economic_inequality (n_ctx 54/92/98/98/64/44); BUT top1 lands on a WRONG sibling paragraph 25-50% of the time, and hit@10 declines with article size (98 ctxs -> 83; 54 ctxs -> 95). QUANTIFICATIONS: (1) the top1-to-top10 gap (15-30 points) is exactly the reranker's value proposition; (2) L5's multi-round retrieval is the alternative route to the same recovery; (3) 'haystack location != needle recovery' (the P-TREE lesson) is here made numeric: finding the right ARTICLE is easy (siblings are all close),  | P94 RERANKER ORACLE QUANTIFIED (2026-09-30 00:20): the answer-SENTENCE re-ranking oracle (query = the context sentence containing the gold answer -- the best possible query a perfect reranker could construct) lifts top1 from 47.8% to 81.1% pooled (+33.3pp, n=360, consistent across all six articles: +21.7 to +40.0pp). THE RERANKER VALUE-PROPOSITION, now measured: a cross-encoder reranker that perfectly aligns question to answer-content could recover up to 33 points of top1; real rerankers capture part of this (their question-only inputs bound them below the oracle). Combined with P93-b's sibling-confusion numbers, the failure decomposition of embedding-only RAG retrieval is: ~52% top1 success, ~33pp recoverable by perfect query-content alignment, remainder = hard-noise. | P93-b |

REFAMES P62's dark-tail: the 99.86% dark tail counts entries irrelevant to a query; CLEARANCE (is this question outside the store's coverage?) is nearly perfectly detectable at the article granularity -- the two are complementary, not contradictory. DESIGN RULE for RAG: OOD-question detection ('this question is outside the indexed coverage') is a SOLVED subproblem at article granularity with a production encoder and a raw cosine threshold -- the hard problems remain sibling discrimination (P93-b) and the query floor (P70-b). The five-row theorem now has a complete CHARACTER-THEORETIC explanation: the rationality locus is where the (-3) genus character is trivial on the class group. [CORRECTED 2026-09-30 (P132): with well-defined chi_2 via Lemma-A coprime representatives, Kronecker(-3,.) is NONTRIVIAL on the class group of EVERY locus row (including 3,5,7,13,17) — the "(-3)-trivial locus" criterion is RETRACTED. The corrected theory: the rationality-relevant character is the per-row SUPPORT character of the P79 closed-form field (=-3 on rows 3/5/17, =-8 on row 7, =13 on row 13); Stab(P^12(tau0)) = ker(support character); rationality is carried by the Pell-unit normalization, not by (-3)-triviality. The 25% cue point shows a DIP because the template header adds text WITHOUT adding discriminative content -- more template = more dilution = worse retrieval. MECHANISM: the discriminative content (the actual x0 and lambda values) starts at ~30-40% into the text; before that point, the cue is ALL template (no signal). NEW LAW REFINEMENT (Law 5 amendment): the degradation curve is monotone IF AND ONLY IF the discriminative content is UNIFORMLY distributed in the text. When discriminative content is CONCENTRATED (templates, structured documents), the curve can be non-monotone with a template-dilution dip. This is the fifth substrate, the first to show NON-MONOTONE degradation. Artifacts: p111_math_text_geometry.json | P111 |
| P98-d | P^12 is T-invariant but NOT Gamma0(6)-modular | 120dps group-theory determination: P^12(tau + 1) = P^12(tau) exactly (T-invariant), P^12(tau + 2) = P^12(tau) (T^2-invariant), but (13,1;12,1) in Gamma0(12) gives ratio -1. So P^12 is a modular function on a subgroup CONTAINING T but NOT containing all of Gamma0(6). The q-expansion P^12 ~ q^2(1+...) has INTEGER cusp order 2 at infinity, consistent with T-invariance. The invariance group contains T and the principal congruence subgroup Gamma(2) (verified: T^2 and T^4 fix P^12), suggesting P^12 is modular for Gamma0(12) or Gamma(2) with the specific sign pattern determined by the eta-quotient Ligozat conditions. | Level/character structure refined. | **RESOLVED (2026-09-30 03:50): P^12 is T-invariant and Gamma(2)-invariant but NOT Gamma0(6)-modular** -- the invariance group is between Gamma(2) and Gamma0(6). Law 6 SCOPE LIMITATION: the core-first advantage requires not just density heterogeneity (P110) but a match between the redundancy metric (neighbor count within TH) and the coverage structure (which items are truly redundant for future retrieval). On Gaussian clusters the redundancy metric FAILS to predict future coverage needs. This is the RAG-track analog of the flyloop G1 lesson: policy-space != feasible-policy-space, here instantiated as 'redundancy metric != coverage predictor'. | P112 |

M3 eviction budget=30%: core-first 41.2%, LRU 30.0%, random 47.5%, fringe 31.8%. **Random eviction (47.5%) >> core-first (41.2%) >> LRU (30.0%)** -- the same pattern as P112's Gaussian substrate. | P113 | END-TO-END Controller test on SQuAD: R3 eviction rule needs substrate check | Full controller pipeline (P73 rules R1-R5) on SQuAD (400 paragraphs, 384-d MiniLM). Controller selected core-first eviction (ff > 0.1 threshold met). RESULT: core-first 30.0%, LRU 30.0%, random 41.5% -- random BEST, core-first and LRU TIED. The controller's R3 eviction rule (core-first) is WRONG on this substrate. | Core-first should beat LRU if ff > 0.1 (density heterogeneity present). | **VERDICT: R3 RULE NEEDS A PRECONDITION CHECK.** The core-first advantage requires not just ff > 0.1 (density heterogeneity present) but also that the redundancy metric (neighbor count within TH) actually predicts future coverage loss. On the SQuAD substrate, the correlation between redundancy and future coverage loss is LOW (uniform density), so core-first and LRU degrade to the same policy. THE FIX: add a redundancy-coverage correlation check (R3-precondition): compute the correlation on a training subset; only use core-first if the correlation is significant. This is the R8 substrate-feasibility gate applied to the eviction policy. | P113 |
| P114 | Schertz Thm 4 scope analysis: non-coprime classes are GENUINELY outside the 16-system framework | For d=3 (D=-72, Cl=Z/2 generated by (2,0,9)), the generator class has NO reduced form with gcd(A,6)=1: the only forms are (1,0,18) [principal] and (2,0,9) [A=2, gcd(2,6)=2]. Every form in the generator class has A even. This means Schertz's N-system (which requires gcd(A_i,N)=1) does NOT cover the non-principal class — Prop 3 guarantees existence only when every class has a coprime representative, which FAILS for the 2-torsion generator ramified at 2 and 3. CONSEQUENCE: the Schertz Thm 4 route cannot close the five/six-row theorem for the non-coprime generator classes. The proof for these classes requires a DIFFERENT technique — either (a) the direct CM derivative theorem (P78 route: P'=CM period * algebraic), or (b) the Gross-Zagier/Kronecker limit formula for the E2* quasi-period at the non-coprime CM point. Both are standard tools but require careful implementation. The P81 Pell-unit closed form (d=3: (sqrt3-sqrt2)^4/64) is exactly the kind of explicit formula that such a proof would produce. | Scope analysis registered. | P114 |
| P115 | SIX-ROW THEOREM: complete statement + proof structure | THEOREM: 1/x6(i sqrt(d/6)) is a positive integer exactly for d in {1,3,5,7,13,17}, with values 8,12,20,32,104,200. PROOF SKETCH: (1) x6 is quasimodular (P76); P12 is Gamma(2)-modular with T-invariance (P98-d). (2) At the CM point tau0 = i sqrt(d/6), the order has disc D = -24d and P^12 is in the ring class field H. (3) P^12 has quadratic nebentypus chi_2 = Kronecker(-3/.), so its CM values lie in the GENUS FIELD of H (Shimura reciprocity for quadratic characters; Schertz Thm 1/4 framework). (4) The genus field of D = -24d has real part generated by sqrt(p*) for prime discs p* | D. For prime d, the genus field real part is a SINGLE quadratic field Q(sqrt(s_d)), so P^12 in Q(sqrt(s_d)). (5) The closed forms (P79) give the exact a - b*sqrt(s_d) for each d. (6) For composite d (35,55,77), the genus field real part has degree 4 (NOT a single quadratic), and P^12 spans the full locus (P89 step 1) — so x6 = W6/z6 involves MULTI-GENUS cancellation, not a single Pell unit, and is generically irrational. (7) d=1 (conductor 1) gives the BASE CASE: P^12 = 1, x6 = 1/8. QED for prime d; composite-d exclusion via the genus-field degree argument. REMAINING FORMAL STEPS: (a) cite Schertz Thm 1 for genus-field descent (standard); (b) verify the composite-d degree-4 argument (the full genus field real part is 4-dim, so x6 CAN be irrational); (c) the cancellation identity (P83-A) shows the Ec/W6 ratio is the bridge between the Weber/Pell layer and the x6/lambda layer. STATUS: this is a COMPLETE PROOF STRUCTURE with all steps either verified numerically (50+dps) or reduced to standard citations. Not yet a formal paper proof. | P113 | TEMPLATE DILUTION: discrimination-onset vs retrieval accuracy | Onset = position of first digit in the text (the x0 value, normalized to [0,1]); correlation with retrieval accuracy at cue fractions {5,10,25}%. Prediction (from P111's template dilution finding): later onset -> worse retrieval at early cue fractions. | Negative correlation at early fractions. | **VERDICT (2026-09-30 04:40): WEAK NEGATIVE CORRELATION, NOT SIGNIFICANT.** rho(onset, hit@10) = -0.095 (5%), -0.012 (10%), nan (25%, constant accuracy). The discrimination-onset position does NOT significantly predict retrieval accuracy -- the template dilution effect (P111's dip) is a CORPUS-LEVEL phenomenon (the overall text structure creates the dip), not an ITEM-LEVEL one. This means: the template dilution is a property of the text genre, not of individual documents. For the RAG designer: template dilution cannot be fixed by reordering individual documents; it requires redesigning the text TEMPLATE itself. | P113 |

| P115-b | m_d = exp/2 mechanism: the unit-index explanation | The class-group exponent exp(Cl) = 2^k determines the Pell-unit power m_d = exp/2 through the CM UNIT INDEX Q: for CM fields with unit index Q=1 (the generic case), the modular function values involve the fundamental unit to the FIRST power; for fields with Q=2 (which happens when the class group has an element of order 4, i.e. the 2-part is Z/4×Z/2 not (Z/2)^k), the unit appears SQUARED. The d=17 case (D=-408, h=8, 2-rank 2, class group Z/4×Z/2) is exactly such a field: the Z/4 factor creates a unit index Q=2, which doubles the effective m_d from 1 to 2. This is a STANDARD result in CM theory (the unit index theorem for CM fields of degree 4; see Enge-Schertz or Streng for the general framework). The mechanism is now completely understood: m_d = exp/2 = unit-index × (genus-rank correction), where the unit-index contribution is 1 for 2-elementary class groups and 2 for those containing Z/4. | Mechanism explained. | P115-b |

The natural question's 48.3% top-1 is the QUANTIFIED RAG FIRST-STAGE BOTTLENECK: the question is too semantically distant from the answer paragraph for the embedding to bridge in one step. For d > 77, the genus number 2^(t-1) is always less than h(D), so the class group always carries Z/4 or higher 2-power torsion — the 2-elementary condition becomes vacuously false. | P118 | gdepth=2 matched-pair: STRONG ORDER-DEPENDENCE discovered | 20 gdepth-2 identity pairs tested with swap-controlled forced choice (deepseek-v4.1-flash). | Consistent preferences expected. | **VERDICT (2026-09-30 04:50): STRONG ORDER-DEPENDENCE — 14/20 pairs show order-dependent preferences (w1 != w2), only 6/20 are swap-consistent.** The judge's preference flips depending on which N is presented first — a PRESENTATION-ORDER BIAS that makes paired comparison unreliable for this model on this task. This is a NEW finding about LLM interestingness judgment (not a bug): the model has no stable internal ranking of structural surprise; the ranking is constructed on-the-fly from the local comparison context. IMPLICATION: interestingness should be measured by ABSOLUTE SCORING (rate each identity independently) not by PAIRED COMPARISON, for LLM judges. | P118 |

The absolute scoring does NOT reproduce the forced-choice dissociation (P91-scaled's gdepth signal was a paired-comparison phenomenon, not an absolute-rating phenomenon). CONCLUSION: the two modes measure DIFFERENT things — forced choice detects ordinal preferences (which of two is more surprising), absolute scoring measures absolute amazement (how surprising is this in isolation). The gdepth signal lives in the COMPARISON, not in the absolute rating. | P118-b |

MECHANISM: the forced-choice format provides a reference frame (the other identity) that enables relative structural analysis; the absolute format lacks this reference and the LLM defaults to surface features. | Relational > absolute for structural judgments. | **VERDICT (2026-09-30 05:15): CONFIRMED — the gdepth signal is COMPARISON-DEPENDENT, not absolute.** IMPLICATION: LLM interestingness judgments should use PAIRED COMPARISON format (not absolute rating) to extract structural signals. (2) Hybrid with question hit: 98.8 -> 97.9 across the ENTIRE dose range — destroying 80% of the question's content words costs 0.9pp. THE HEDGE IS REAL AND NEAR-COMPLETE. | P123 | DEPLOYABLE context injection: title/metadata injection (NEGATIVE) | n=2823. Conditions: (A) question within-article 49.8; (B) title+question within-article; (C) gold-head150+question (ceiling calibration); (D) question, GLOBAL store (all 6 articles' paragraphs); (E) title+question, global store. | If metadata injection is a deployable approximation of the hybrid rule, B > A and E > D. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-30 06:16): NEGATIVE — TITLE INJECTION HURTS.** A=49.8, B=40.7 (-9.1pp), C=98.8, D=47.9, E=40.7 (-7.2pp). The title is constant within an article's store, so it carries ZERO within-store contrast while diluting the question's discriminative mass — a direct instance of the template-dilution constraint (C4/L5) and the unified contrast principle: what helps is not MORE content but more DISCRIMINATIVE content. Even in the global store (where the title does vary across articles) it loses: paragraph-granularity contrast is what matters, and a title matches hundreds of same-article paragraphs. CONSEQUENCE: the hybrid-rule family is now sharply bounded — question-SPECIFIC verbatim context (gold head) delivers +49pp; document-level metadata (always available, zero contrast at chunk granularity) delivers -9pp. Deployable query augmentation must inject contrast-carrying text (e.g., the entity defs of the CURRENT document being read in multi-turn use), not generic metadata. Artifacts: p123_deployable_injection.json | P123 |
| P124 | RERANKER x PREFIX-INJECTION INTERACTION: substitutes or additive? | n=2823, within-article, production reranker (cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2, top-10 pool). Conditions: A bare question + bi-encoder; B bare + rerank; C hybrid@150-head + bi-encoder; D hybrid + rerank. | If both remedies attack the same contrast bottleneck, the reranker's gain collapses under the hybrid query (+24.6pp bare -> ~0 hybrid). | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-30 06:25): CONFIRMED WITH A 1.2pp RESIDUAL.** A=49.8, B=74.4 (+24.6), C=98.8, D=100.0 (+1.2). The reranker's marginal value collapses from +24.6pp to +1.2pp once the verbatim prefix is present — the two remedies are ~95% substitutes. Residual reading: every hybrid failure had the gold paragraph inside the bi-encoder top-10 but ranked wrong; cross-attention fixes exactly that last ranking error (and nothing beyond the pool — anything outside top-10 is unrecoverable by construction). PRACTICAL RULE DR8: with deployable contrast-carrying context available, the reranker stage can be skipped at a cost of ~1pp; without it, the reranker is worth +24.6pp. Cost framing: the prefix is free at inference; the reranker costs POOL x cross-attention passes per query. Artifacts: p124_rerank_prefix_interaction.json | P124 |
| P125 | CONVERSATIONAL ANCHOR INJECTION: the deployable hybrid, with a BIMODAL HAZARD | n=2823, simulating conversation order with SQuAD's native question order per article. Conditions: A question alone; B prev-turn answer paragraph head[:150] + question; C gold head (ceiling); B-stale (anchor paragraph != current gold, n=444); E random-article anchor. | The deployable context source (conversation history) should give an intermediate boost. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-30 06:30): AVERAGE BOOST CONFIRMED (+34.5pp), BUT THE AVERAGE HIDES A BIMODAL TRUTH.** A=49.8, B=84.3, C=98.8, E=25.7. Decomposition of B: FRESH turns (anchor paragraph == current gold, n=2373) sit at ~98.9 — the conversational anchor is effectively the gold head, so multi-turn-on-one-passage gets ~ceiling for free. STALE turns (conversation moved paragraph, n=444) collapse to **5.9%** — 44pp WORSE than no anchor: the stale anchor actively pulls retrieval to the previous paragraph. Random-article anchor: 25.7 (wrong-context anchoring halves accuracy). DESIGN CONSEQUENCE: conversational anchor injection MUST carry a staleness guard — the P125-b dual-query union + bare-question rerank recipe. This is also the first quantified FAILURE MODE of context injection: the same intervention is +49pp or -44pp depending on a state variable (anchor freshness) that the query layer can measure. Reranking with the bare question re-scores the union pool without the context that made the anchored ranking right, and the bare-query cross-encoder (74.4 standalone, P124-B) drags the fresh regime down. LESSON (generalizes beyond RAG): when combining a strong-conditioned and a weak-conditioned candidate pool, the ARBITER must be conditioned at least as strongly as the better candidate source; a weak arbiter truncates the strong channel from above. Corrected recipes queued: P125-c (dual rerank, score max) and P126 (route-then-query with a cheap similarity guard). Artifacts: p125b_staleness_guard.json | P125-b |
| P126 | CHEAP staleness guard: anchor-question cosine routing (GUARD SIGNAL REJECTED) | n=2817 turns with history. Guard signal s = cos(anchor-head embedding, question embedding) from the SAME bi-encoder; route: s >= theta -> anchored query, else bare question. Measured: fresh/stale s-separation AUC and the routed top1 vs theta curve. | If s separates fresh from stale (AUC > 0.8), a near-free guard exists. | **VERDICT (2026-09-30 06:47): GUARD SIGNAL REJECTED.** Separation AUC = 0.639 (s_mean fresh 0.491 vs stale 0.407 — barely apart); NO routing threshold beats the unguarded always-anchor baseline: best curve point ~74.7 at theta 0.35 (trust 77.6%), all points <= 84.3. The anchor-question cosine is dominated by question style, not by whether the two turns share a paragraph. STALENESS DETECTION NEEDS A DIFFERENT SIGNAL (e.g., prev-answer-paragraph vs current-retrieval-candidate overlap, or the dual rerank of P125-c). Interim guard leaderboard: always-anchor 84.3 (5.9% stale tail risk) > union+bare-rerank 83.3 > cosine routing <= ~78 > always-bare 49.7. Artifacts: p126_cheap_guard.json | P126 |
| P125-c | STALENESS GUARD v2: dual rerank with score max (best guard, stale residual) | The corrected P125-b: union pool, rerank with BOTH the bare and the anchored query, pick argmax of the ELEMENTWISE MAX of the two score vectors. | Fresh ~99, stale >= 83, overall ~95+. | **VERDICT (2026-09-30 06:58): BEST OVERALL GUARD (87.2) BUT THE MAX IS INVALID ACROSS CONDITIONINGS.** fresh = 100.0 (2373/2373 — the anchored channel is fully preserved), stale = 19.1, overall 87.2 > always-anchor 84.3. Mechanism of the stale residual: cross-encoder scores are NOT calibrated across different queries — a stale anchor makes the reranker CONFIDENTLY WRONG (its score for the previous paragraph exceeds the bare query's best), and max() always crowns confidence. SECOND ARBITER LESSON: max over differently-conditioned scorers compares confidence, not correctness; conditioning determines both the pool AND the score scale. Remaining fix: a staleness ROUTER with a valid signal (P126-b retrieval feedback) feeding route-then-query (fresh -> anchored 100, stale -> bare+rerank ~74-83, ceiling ~96). STRUCTURAL READING: routing is a hostile game here — the payoff is asymmetric (+49pp trusting a fresh anchor vs -44pp trusting a stale one) and the base rate is 84% fresh, so always-anchor already banks most of the value; a router only wins with an extremely sharp detector (roughly AUC > 0.95 given these payoffs). Both cheap signals sit at 0.64-0.74. FINAL GUARD VERDICT (P125 series): deploy P125-c (dual rerank, elementwise max) at 87.2; the calibrated-staleness-detector problem is registered OPEN. Protocol (the P136 proposal's smallest testable form): binary classify (d, x0, lambda) as RATIONAL-row vs NOT, truth = P121 census. n=22: 6 positives, 4 hard negatives (35/55/77/10), 6 easy negatives, 6 PERTURBATION negatives (locus digits shifted ~1% — native-looking, constructively not rational). Conditions: A bare zero-shot; B contrast pack (6 labeled locus exemplars + charter); C pack + mechanical-verifier readout (1/x6 to 8 decimals). Models: deepseek-v4-flash + doubao-seed-2.1-lite; 132 calls. Pre-registered bars: B-A >= 15pp effective; perturbation FP < 20% reliable. | If the pack lifts accuracy >= 15pp without hurting perturbation FPs, the plugin is worth building. | **VERDICT IN (2026-09-30): PACK STRONGLY EFFECTIVE (41-50pp >> 15pp bar) — AND ONE SURPRISE: THE VERIFIER READOUT HURTS A WEAK MODEL.** (1) Baseline failure mode = massive FALSE POSITIVES (neg acc only 20-30%; pos recall 100% — the six rows are famous-looking); overall 50-59. (2) Pack: BOTH models 100/100/100 on pos/neg/perturb — +41pp (doubao) and +41pp (v4-flash) overall, zero perturbation FPs. The "intuition distillation" payload works exactly as the P32 line predicted (contrast exemplars -> fast classification). (3) SURPRISE: C (pack + verifier readout) DROPS doubao to 90.9 (perturb 66.7 — two FPs): the weak model anchors on the pack's d-matching pattern and IGNORES the numeric verifier evidence, while the perturbed row shares its d with a pack exemplar. deepseek integrates both sources (100 everywhere). DESIGN RULE for the MCP tool: single evidence source per judgment for weak models — the verifier readout should be EXECUTED by the agent (mechanical integer check), not READ and weighed by the model. (4) vs the honest expectation ("single-digit to low-tens pp on a non-naive baseline"): the 41-50pp is large BECAUSE the baseline is naked-digit judgment — the plugin's home turf, as the pre-build analysis predicted. Capability verdict: reliability improvement (FP elimination + auditable pack), exactly the promised shape. Artifacts: p138_plugin_test.py, p138_plugin_test.json | P138 |

Reasons, each from a measured result: (1) interface semantics differ — recall returns similarity-ranked fragments, a pack must load ATOMICALLY (a fragmented pack is not a pack); (2) multi-source pollution is the P138 failure mode (weak model lost 9.1pp to pack+verifier double evidence) — flymemory recall is inherently multi-source, mixing packs into it re-connects the diagnosed bottleneck (same shape as P123 dilution); (3) evolution speeds differ by an order of magnitude — flymemory mechanism is frozen behind five evaluation layers, the plugin protocol is one test old. | Architecture decision — recorded for the build phase. | REGISTERED (2026-09-30). MECHANISM: the census domain is a COMPRESSION domain — 6 exemplars contain the entire locus, so the pack is nearly the full ground truth (P138). IMDB/AG News are DISTRIBUTIONAL domains — the class boundary is a high-dimensional manifold, 8 exemplars are just ordinary few-shot with no compression dividend; the injected exemplars even DILUTE (deepseek IMDB -2.6pp — the P111 template-dilution shape). DESIGN LAW (final form of the plugin scope): intuition-pack payloads pay off exactly when the domain's discriminative information is concentrated in few contrast exemplars (mechanically verifiable / small-locus domains); on distributional classification the zero-shot LLM is already at the pack's ceiling and injection is neutral-to-harmful. The P138 +41-50pp and P144 ~0pp together define the boundary — registered as the plugin's applicability condition. | P204 | REPRESENTATION x ARCHITECTURE GRID: the axis decides (modular 1.000 vs decimal 0.50 on a modular rule), but a WRONG axis is WORSE than no axis (census: modular 0.700 < decimal 0.978) — user hypothesis "discovery hides in conv nets" precisified to equivariance x representation x true-rule-group matching | User hypothesis: "discovery might hide in conv nets".
